The combined model is stricter about route state and ethics: shortness cannot hide material facts; pictures and alternative text do not by themselves create equivalent selection; backtracking may be exploration; age is not a behavior proxy; and first-position effects require fair, user-serving ordering rather than exploitation. Deep links, history, permissions, authentication, localization, dynamic availability, privacy-preserving analytics, external handoffs, and current accessibility behavior remain explicit gaps.

Chapter 6 corroborates the completed books on modular structured content, user-centered taxonomy, explicit policy/procedure relations, multi-route discovery, coherent task scope, responsive semantic parity, and purpose-led formats. Its strongest addition is the separation of canonical information from inherited document and page boundaries: one semantic source can support whole-work, topical, print, mobile, social, HTML, and PDF projections when their relations and versions are governed. The combined model rejects the chapter's fixed scroll limit and any assumption that smaller screens require less meaning; it treats questions, roles, product types, life events, and sequence as testable overlapping views rather than population truth. Document conversion remains a records-and-migration operation, not permission to discard authoritative sources, while current PDF, accessibility, SEO, and runtime contracts remain unverified because the required Developer Index is quota-limited.

Chapter 7 corroborates the completed books on question-led requirements, unresolved-fact escalation, proposition-preserving reduction, key-message priority, progressive detail, semantic structure, comprehension testing, and cross-channel lineage. Its useful “bite, snack, meal” model becomes three governed projections over canonical meaning rather than permission to tease or conceal. The combined model is stricter about relevance and evidence: strategy fit, persona imagination, word-count reduction, F-pattern attention, conversion, and clicks cannot establish user need or benefit; unasked disclosures, rights, risk, uncertainty, exceptions, and minority needs remain nonoptional. Overlays, tabs, and progressive disclosure require current semantics, focus, keyboard, expanded-state, deep-link, print, search, localization, and fallback behavior that the 2012 Show/Hide example does not prove.

Interlude 2 corroborates the completed books on completing a user-initiated purpose before optional promotion, matching route promises, separating facts from organization-centered copy, and preserving relevant next actions. Its “marketing moment” becomes a governed relevance-and-timing hypothesis rather than inferred readiness to buy. The combined model is much stricter about consent and autonomy: visits, follows, accounts, and purchases do not authorize profiling or cross-channel contact; recommendations need provenance and sponsorship; convenience and urgency require protection against accidental or pressured action; financial and necessary services may prohibit cross-sell; chat, feedback, social media, and contact are distinct operated channels; and successful exit is equal to continued engagement.

Chapter 8 corroborates the completed books on meaningful link purpose, audience vocabulary, key-message priority, semantic markup, route/destination parity, and decision-supporting descriptions. Its strongest contribution is treating the headline as a portable promise that may appear without its page. The combined model separates page title, primary heading, route label, link text, accessible name, social title, and search title while requiring intentional parity. It rejects fixed word counts, odd-number formulas, punctuation patterns, raw click-through, and keyword insertion as quality rules; treats statement, question, action, category, warning, status, and record titles as semantic types; and replaces a single “global audience” with locale-specific concept mapping, transcreation, casing, wrapping, pronunciation, and testing.

Chapter 9 corroborates the completed books on outline-first structure, question-to-answer lineage, meaningful labels, task language, proposition-first sections, semantic markup, and accessibility as implemented behavior. Its strongest addition is using the heading-only outline as structural lint: difficulty naming, duplicate headings, or incoherent flow points back to mixed or scattered content. The combined model distinguishes heading form from semantic rank and visual token; allows real hierarchy beyond an arbitrary H3 cap; makes reusable components accept contextual levels; treats mobile as another projection of the same meaning; types statements as claims and imperatives as executable affordances; and turns in-page links into focus, URL, history, offset, motion, localization, print, and synchronization contracts. Historical medical, privacy, SEO, typography, color, placement, and persona-channeling claims remain bounded examples rather than rules.

Interlude 3 corroborates the completed books on web-native structure, key-message summaries, source linking, audience-specific channels, multimodal content, cross-surface projections, and evaluation. Its distinctive contribution is recognizing that a release escapes its original media pitch and becomes persistent public information. The combined model makes that persistence governable: releases need canonical identity, explicit genre and currency, typed contacts and evidence links, quotation provenance, media rights and alternatives, publication/modified/correction dates, embargo and release states, corrections and retractions, syndicated-copy handling, archive/tombstone policy, machine metadata, and accessible print. Search attention, social pickup, and a 15-of-16 preference result remain bounded discovery or formative signals rather than quality proof.

Chapter 10 corroborates the completed books on direct address, imperative instructions, actor clarity, active voice, plain language, concise structure, audience vocabulary, and evidence-based revision. Its distinctive contribution is grammatical diagnosis as interaction diagnosis: pronouns expose roles, voice exposes responsibility, subject–verb–object separation exposes tangled propositions, given-before-new ordering exposes premature action, and rewriting can reveal missing operational facts. The combined model is stricter than the chapter's stylistic heuristics: “you” and “we” need typed referents; imperatives need executable and safety contracts; passive voice remains valid in context; cuts require semantic diff; fragments and word counts are not universal; modality preserves authority; and regulated examples require authoritative review. Gender guidance is historically binary, localization is explicitly out of scope, and current speech, cognitive-accessibility, machine-translation, safety, legal, and inclusive-language contracts remain unverified while the Developer Index quota is exhausted.

Chapter 11 corroborates the completed books on semantic structure, procedure design, explicit choices, progressive task decomposition, responsive parity, quantitative clarity, and programmatic accessibility. Its distinctive contribution is a compact relational grammar: bullets denote unordered sets or alternatives, numbers denote sequence, and tables relate two or more categories. The combined model adds the contracts the chapter leaves implicit—selection cardinality, stable step state, branch logic, safety timing, captions and scoped headers, units and range boundaries, provenance, locale and reading direction, keyboard and speech navigation, responsive transformation, and export/print equivalence. Fixed list lengths, two-column preference, no-horizontal-scroll advice, age-based distraction claims, and styling remain bounded heuristics. The suggestion to put a map's option list in an alt attribute is rejected because description is not an operable equivalent interface; current external implementation contracts remain unverified because the Developer Index interface is unavailable.

Interlude 4 corroborates the completed books on plain language, direct actor naming, meaningful headings, layered definitions, proposition-preserving transformation, dates, in-page structure, and operated contact paths. Its strongest contribution is treating accuracy, sufficiency, and clarity as compatible but independently reviewable qualities. The combined model is much stricter than its 2012 privacy-policy example: clarity cannot prove legality, fairness, consent, enforceability, trust, or practice parity; legal citations and explanations need authority lineage; policies need full version and change lifecycle; and every collection, purpose, recipient, cookie, child, control, right, and remedy claim must derive from current systems and jurisdiction. The sample's absolute collection and sharing claims, contact-based marketing refusal, simplified cookie account, and single under-13 rule are historical risks, not reusable language. Current primary contracts remain unverified because the Developer Index interface is unavailable.

Chapter 12 corroborates the completed books on meaningful link purpose, action-object labels, route/destination parity, extracted-context accessibility, coherent link sets, and recovery from wrong turns. Its strongest contribution is the “launch and land” contract: people should be able to predict a destination before activation and confirm it afterward. The combined model expands that beyond matching strings to canonical identity, account and tenant state, role, locale, record, permission, destination type, commitment, external ownership, focus, history, preserved context, and safe return. Generic links remain unacceptable when their programmatic context does not distinguish them; inline placement and visited styling are contextual rather than universal; browser history is not domain completion state; and color never carries affordance alone. Security, redirects, referrer exposure, downloads, phishing, sensitive URLs, logging, expiry, voice control, and current browser/accessibility behavior are explicit gaps while the Developer Index interface is unavailable.

Chapter 13 corroborates the completed books on purpose-led media, user-question lineage, procedural and data structure, contextual interpretation testing, truthful representation, performance, motion restraint, and accessible alternatives. Its distinctive contribution is separating exact representation, self-service, process, data, and mood as different media purposes. The combined model derives different contracts from those purposes: product images need claim parity; diagrams need operable equivalence; process media need stable step state; charts need data and provenance; mood and fundraising imagery need claim and manipulation review; representation needs consent and anti-tokenism; screenshots need privacy and lifecycle controls; and animation needs user control plus a static equivalent. Alt text is one layer rather than a universal solution, and captions, transcripts, audio description, structured data, responsive art direction, rights, authenticity, synthetic-media disclosure, localization, security, and post-publication maintenance remain essential. The chapter's file-size, SEO, zero-baseline, and demographic heuristics are historically bounded, while current technical contracts remain unverified because the Developer Index interface is unavailable.

Chapter 14 corroborates the completed books on iterative writing, claim provenance, link verification, early feedback, role-specific review, structured comprehension checks, specialist collaboration, and explicit disposition of requested changes. Its distinctive contribution is review design: copy editing and developmental editing answer different questions; reviewers need stage- and role-specific requests; factual conflicts require investigation rather than aggregation; and accepted, rejected, or modified comments need rationale across rounds. The combined model is stricter than personal diligence: two sets of eyes are not assurance, read-aloud is not accessibility testing, persona walkthroughs are not participant evidence, comments are self-selected, consistency cannot erase authorship, color cannot identify reviewers alone, and compromise cannot change controlled truth or rights. Canonical versions, accessible structured findings, automated and manual release gates, localization, security, privacy, resolved-runtime validation, correction propagation, rollback, monitoring, and audit remain required; current platform contracts are unverified while the Developer Index interface is unavailable.

Interlude 5 corroborates the completed books on modular content, accountable ownership, findability, examples, usability testing, feedback, terminology consistency, and lifecycle maintenance. Its distinctive contribution is a deliberately small style guide that behaves like an internal product: start with real decisions, give each topic a card, expose search and links, and grow through questions. The combined model makes “organic” governable rather than informal. Every rule needs type, source, scope, precedence, rationale, locale, channel, examples, exceptions, owner, effective date, status, machine-enforcement behavior, feedback disposition, version, migration, deprecation, and historical access. Consistency remains subordinate to truth, accessibility, identity, law, records, and community language; management sponsorship provides resources but not correctness; self-identification outranks committee pronoun preference; and automated fixes require explainability and human review. Current linting and platform contracts remain unverified because the Developer Index interface is unavailable.

Chapter 15 corroborates the completed books on question-led research, realistic tasks, observed behavior, formative iteration, method fit, mixed evidence, accessibility participation, global context, and testing content beyond findability. Its strongest contribution is the separation of talk from action: focus groups, moderated task sessions, unmoderated metrics, remote studies, and field experiments answer different questions, while comprehension and use must be observed after arrival. The combined model rejects method labels and sample counts as assurance. Recruitment needs an explicit coverage matrix; tasks need provenance and safety; facilitation needs consent, accommodation, safeguarding, and incident capability; observation, self-report, telemetry, and inference remain distinct; lightweight studies still require secure data handling; disability research must be disability-led; global research needs local authority and data sovereignty; and A/B conversion needs access, suitability, rights, regret, and harm guardrails. Findings must link to changes, retests, releases, and production monitoring. Current research-platform contracts remain unverified because the Developer Index interface is unavailable.

## Book-level synthesis

1. **Begin with the person's purpose, not the organization's document.** The durable unit is an answer, decision, or action within a real situation. Existing pages and files are sources and records, not automatic experience boundaries.
2. **Treat content, structure, navigation, and behavior as one design problem.** Home, pathway, destination, mobile, print, and social projections must preserve canonical meaning while helping people orient, predict, act, and recover.
3. **Base audience and task models on evidence.** Interviews, observation, analytics, search, support data, surveys, scenarios, and personas contribute different evidence. Personas and scenarios remain hypotheses; low frequency never erases high-consequence people or obligations.
4. **Operate content as a governed lifecycle.** Strategy requires purpose, inventory, ownership, canonical sources, review, publication, maintenance, correction, retirement, migration, archive, and cross-channel lineage—not merely a launch calendar.
5. **Make hierarchy and language expose the interaction.** Headings, questions, statements, imperatives, pronouns, voice, links, lists, tables, and media should clarify proposition, actor, choice, sequence, relation, destination, consequence, and next step.
6. **Use plain, conversational language without fabricating reciprocity.** Direct address and an approachable voice can reduce distance, but “conversation” must not imply that a system listened, understood, cared, or possessed human agency. Institutional actors and accountability stay explicit.
7. **Reduce and layer without deleting material truth.** Key-message-first structure, progressive detail, summaries, and modular projections are valuable only when qualifications, exceptions, uncertainty, rights, risk, records, and authoritative source access remain available.
8. **Subordinate marketing and business outcomes to autonomy and suitability.** Promotion after task progress is at most a relevance hypothesis. Conversion, engagement, return visits, sales, support reduction, and SEO cannot outweigh informed choice, refusal, successful exit, privacy, safety, accessibility, or public obligation.
9. **Test the full meaning-and-action journey with method fit.** Findability, comprehension, interpretation, decision quality, execution, recovery, and outcomes are distinct. Qualitative studies, focus groups, analytics, and experiments answer different questions and require ethical recruitment, consent, accommodation, data governance, and bounded inference.
10. **Modernize every 2012 implementation claim before adoption.** Accessibility, responsive behavior, mobile, browser history, SEO, privacy, security, localization, inclusive language, legal content, research tooling, and media alternatives require current primary standards, runtime validation, and jurisdiction-specific authority.

The book's distinctive value is its unusually complete bridge from content strategy and information architecture to page structure, sentence craft, media choice, review, style guidance, and testing. Its limitations are equally important: it is a 2012, predominantly English-language and desktop-web account; some disability, gender, privacy, legal, SEO, conversion, browser, and device framing is dated; many recommendations are practitioner heuristics illustrated by a nonrepresentative historical website corpus; and the conversation metaphor can conceal asymmetry or automation. The combined model should retain the task-oriented systems view while adding current accessibility, localization, privacy, security, rights, evidence, records, and harm governance.

## Disposition audit

All 588 records are accounted for below. “Retain” means retain as a bounded candidate for cross-book synthesis; it does not authorize implementation.

| Record range | Disposition | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `LGW-C01`–`LGW-C10` | Retain with provenance and metaphor limits | Establishes exact-copy identity, scope, foreword positioning, introduction, and the task/conversation frame; recoded-file authenticity and metaphorical agency remain bounded. |
| `LGW-C11`–`LGW-C27` | Retain as purpose-and-task foundation | Strongly supports user-purpose, answer/action, search, navigation, and evaluation framing; self-service, scanning, engagement, and business alignment are not universal goals. |
| `LGW-C28`–`LGW-C66` | Retain with research-governance expansion | Audience research, questions, goals, scenarios, and personas are useful inputs; privacy, consent, representation, uncertainty, and decision-authority controls are required. |
| `LGW-C67`–`LGW-C95` | Retain as lifecycle foundation | Content strategy, inventory, roles, design integration, and maintenance are durable; URL/page centrality, consistency, and business sponsorship do not establish truth or authority. |
| `LGW-C96`–`LGW-C134` | Retain with current accessibility validation | Content-aware visual design and semantic grouping are strong; fixed typography, layout, demographic, color, and device heuristics remain contextual. |
| `LGW-C135`–`LGW-C166` | Retain as orientation and entry-contract candidates | Purpose, identity, task priority, search, and forms are useful; trust, route state, data minimization, refusal, accessibility, and runtime behavior need stronger contracts. |
| `LGW-C167`–`LGW-C190` | Retain as pathway-design candidates | Predictive routes and decision-supporting labels are durable; first-position, image, click-count, and backtracking heuristics require ethical and contextual testing. |
| `LGW-C191`–`LGW-C223` | Retain as structured-content foundation | Canonical information should support multiple projections; document conversion must preserve authoritative records, semantics, dependencies, and equivalent meaning. |
| `LGW-C224`–`LGW-C258` | Retain with material-information safeguards | Question-led scope, key messages, cutting, layering, and comprehension testing are useful; word counts, attention patterns, and inferred relevance cannot remove obligations or risk information. |
| `LGW-C259`–`LGW-C276` | Bound marketing claims; retain autonomy constraints | Task completion before optional promotion is useful; marketing moments, conversion, urgency, profiling, and cross-channel contact require consent, suitability, and necessary-service protections. |
| `LGW-C277`–`LGW-C297` | Retain as portable-title candidates | Headlines can act as portable promises; title, heading, route, accessible name, search, and social fields need typed parity rather than fixed formulas or keyword rules. |
| `LGW-C298`–`LGW-C353` | Retain as semantic-structure and public-record candidates | Heading outlines and web-native releases expose structure; hierarchy, corrections, quotations, rights, dates, syndication, and archives need explicit lifecycle contracts. |
| `LGW-C354`–`LGW-C413` | Retain as linguistic and relational diagnostics | Pronouns, voice, propositions, lists, procedures, and tables can reveal interaction structure; stylistic absolutes, binary gender framing, and inaccessible equivalents are not promoted. |
| `LGW-C414`–`LGW-C436` | Retain plain-language goal; constrain legal/privacy examples | Clarity and layering are durable, but the sample privacy and legal formulations are historical and require current system, legal, jurisdictional, consent, rights, and remedy lineage. |
| `LGW-C437`–`LGW-C463` | Retain as launch-and-land foundation | Meaningful links and destination confirmation are strong; security, permissions, account/tenant state, redirects, downloads, history, focus, and safe return require modern runtime contracts. |
| `LGW-C464`–`LGW-C497` | Retain purpose-led media model with stronger governance | Media purpose should determine format and testing; accessibility is more than alt text and requires rights, privacy, provenance, equivalence, localization, performance, and lifecycle controls. |
| `LGW-C498`–`LGW-C548` | Retain as review and style-system foundation | Iterative, role-specific review and small findable guidance are valuable; canonical versions, precedence, accessibility, machine enforcement, disposition, migration, and audit controls are required. |
| `LGW-C549`–`LGW-C584` | Retain as formative-research foundation | Realistic tasks, observed behavior, method fit, and comprehension/use testing are durable; fixed sample counts, conversion, informal ethics, disability framing, and platform assumptions are bounded. |
| `LGW-C585`–`LGW-C588` | Retain as back-matter provenance only | Bibliography, indexes, biography, and marketing paracontent support discovery, navigation, authorship, and historical context—not current authority, validation, weighting, or efficacy claims. |

### Explicit non-promotions after this book

- No book prose, screenshot, illustration, example, fixture, or third-party website content is copied into the repository or authorized for provider upload, training, or redistribution.
- No 2012 claim about SEO, browsers, mobile devices, accessibility, privacy, security, law, analytics, research platforms, social media, or product behavior is treated as current without primary-source verification.
- No persona, conversation metaphor, scanning pattern, fixed word count, list length, heading depth, placement rule, click count, or demographic generalization becomes a universal UX-writing rule.
- No conversion, engagement, sales, return visit, support reduction, traffic, or organizational goal is accepted as sufficient evidence of user benefit or content quality.
- No alt-text-only, color-only, browser-history-only, or visual-layout-only technique is accepted as an accessible or operable equivalent.
- No bibliography entry, index frequency, testimonial, client list, award, or publisher feature claim is treated as independent validation.
- No record in this ledger changes a schema, prompt, runtime, test, fixture, or product behavior. Cross-book synthesis and implementation-specific evidence remain required.

No implementation change is authorized by this completed ledger.

## Open questions

1. Which principles remain durable across current responsive sites, native apps, voice interfaces, AI-mediated answers, and document-heavy services?
2. What evidence distinguishes a user question, observed task, search query, support issue, organization assumption, and generated hypothesis?
3. Which purposes—exploration, creation, play, social connection, monitoring, deliberation, compliance, or record access—do not fit a narrow answer/task model?
